This is her passion, she tells herself again. Food. Feeding people. And if the words of Julia Child have ever rang truer, it is that people who love to eat are the best people. Carol holds onto that sentiment tightly because she hates most other people most of the time.

The waitstaff had been incredibly friendly and eager, but I wish I could sing more praise about the food. While it was good enough, I was never truly wowed. Each dish felt like it was having an identity crisis, unsure it if was French, or Asian, or Italian. I’m sure I also saw something Indian on the menu. Variety is the spice of life and fusion dishes have great potential to stand out but La Ruche felt like it really missed the mark.

    The one saving grace is perhaps the peculiar charm that head chef Carol Sturka exudes. Speaking to her briefly, I felt her personality and passion. Eating her food, I did not. Owner Koumba Diabaté does well to manage his establishment, but something is sorely lacking. While they shot for the moon, they did not land among the stars.

    Carol fumes. This fucking bitch. Every bit of self control she thinks she has is about to go straight out the window. She paces around her house, the house that being head chef at La Ruche and various other restaurants has helped her to afford, quaint as it may be. Her heels dig into the hardwood and her blood is about to boil out of her eyes when Manousos calls her. She declines it. A text follows shortly after.

    Whatever you are thinking of doing…don’t. It’s just one review. Think about Koumba.

    It’s just one review. Carol laughs maniacally into the air. A review from someone who might as well be the Beyoncé of Toronto’s restaurant industry. She is sweaty all of a sudden, heart beating in parts of her body that she didn’t know was possible. Why is her elbow having a pulse?

    She snatches the business card from the coffee table and stares at the phone number on it, hand trembling. With a deep breath, she unlocks the screen, thumb hovering over the keypad. She types in half the phone number and then stops. What is she doing? Is she really going to call this woman and cuss her out for leaving her a two star review and ridiculing her in her own packed dining room?

    No.

    She is going to do worse.

    Because Carol is Carol, and Carol is not exactly known for being demure and soft spoken, she decides she is going to go to Zosia instead because fuck a phone call. She wants to look Polish wishes-she-were Nigella Lawson in the eyes when she tells her to shove her two stars up her fucking ass.

     

    *

     

    Carol rumbles down Bathurst Street on her black and blue motorcycle. Her leather jacket is zipped all the way up to her chin, gloved hands gripping the handlebars tighter than she normally would. Usually she feels free when she’s riding her bike. Today she feels like revving it through a wall.

    She arrives at The Wine Lady in ten minutes flat and finds the perfect parking spot right out front.  Three years of passing this place almost every day and Carol can’t believe she has never once been inside. She pulls her helmet and gloves off and runs her hand through her hair. As she does, a woman passes by and smiles timidly. Carol, despite feeling the rage of a thousand suns, offers her a wink. The woman blushes.

    Maybe she should have rehearsed whatever she had come here to say but she has been operating on pure adrenaline and caffeine since Manousos’ text message and she might not be able to feel her feet right now.

    The sign on the door says they open at 3:00pm. Carol looks at her watch. 2:15pm. She tries her luck anyways, someone has to be inside. The door opens when she pulls on it so she steps in. It’s small. One long oak bar taking up the right side of the room and just an intimate amount of tables on the other side. Cozy. Carol can hear the radio playing from somewhere in the back.

    “Sorry, we’re not open yet!” A voice calls from the same direction as the music. Zosia’s voice.

    Carol braces herself. She has never something done this before and has one flicker of regret before it vanishes.

    Footsteps approach and Zosia appears from around the corner in ripped jeans, Sambas, and a t-shirt with a wolf on it. There is an expensive looking chain around her neck and this is not the same Zosia that had showed up to La Ruche last night. This version is, and Carol hates herself for it, way hotter.

    Zosia freezes, genuinely looks surprised to see Carol standing there. “Oh. Carol. You’re…here.”

    Carol holds back a scoff. She quickly catches a glimpse of a thigh tattoo that flashes through the rips in Zosia’s jeans. Okay. When Zosia does not shoo her out, she takes another step inside. “You didn’t make it very hard to find you.” Her voice is short and curt and whatever words come out of her mouth next will be just as much a surprise to herself as to Zosia.

    “I think I can guess why you’re here.”

    “You think?” She invites herself further inside, setting her helmet onto the bar with a clunk.

    Zosia glances at it and then back to Carol, eyes dragging down her body almost as if she were drinking in the sight of her in all black and leather, blonde hair slightly ruffled from the ride. Like she can’t believe it but that it makes sense that Carol would ride a motorcycle and not drive a Honda Civic. Carol lets her look, almost encourages it. This is fucking insane. Then Zosia looks out the window at the motorcycle parked out front. “You’re too close to the fire hydrant. You’re going to get a ticket.”

    Carol lets the scoff out now. This is what Zosia is concerned with. She looks back once to check on her bike and then settles her gaze onto Zosia. “No I won’t. I won’t be long.”

    “Suit yourself. What can I do for you?”

    “What can you do for me?” Carol’s eyes widen, voice already one notch higher. “What the fuck gives? Two stars, really? What is that? One star per dish you tried?” A pause. “Nothing for the free dessert?”

    Zosia, much to Carol’s dismay, does not react. Instead, she cocks her head slightly and if Carol had not already been staring at her, would have missed the infuriating little smirk that pulls at her lip. She steps forward, closing some distance and casually props herself onto the barstool directly in front of Carol.

    It’s too close for comfort. Carol could and should step back, but she doesn’t. She will not give Zosia any satisfaction. From this close, she notices two things at once.

    One: her irises are different colours. One darker than the other. Just like the dog she had seen on Instagram.

    Two: her perfume. She recognizes the scent immediately, like a Mack truck plowing into her at full speed. She smells like the six months she had spent in Paris in her early twenties working an apprenticeship at a local restaurant while she had spent most evenings hooking up with rich and beautiful French women. It’s Le Labo Vanille 44.

    This wealthy ass motherfucker.

    “I’m not trying to be your enemy here Carol,” Zosia says as she folds her hands in her lap. Her voice is firm and steady, unlike the way Carol feels. The contrast of it only adds insult to injury. “But I’m also not going to lie and coddle you. That is in neither of our interests. You would be surprised at how many chefs think they are special until I tell them they’re not.”

    “Wooooow.” Carol drags it out long and slow for dramatic effect, shoving her hands into her pockets. “You must be up to your neck in thank you cards. Who do you actually think you are? Jesus? Gordon Ramsey?”

    Zosia laughs at that. A delicate, out of place giggle given the circumstance, bubbling out of her and Carol almost softens. “I’m not religious,” she says, as if they were having some friendly get-to-know-you conversation. “Though I do love a thank you card.”

    Carol rolls her eyes right in Zosia’s face and Zosia almost seems amused by that. It’s just a goddamn figure of speech. Now Carol has words stuck in her throat. She had come here looking for a fight but Zosia is not giving her one. A door opens somewhere in the back followed by a scuffle of footsteps and then a very young man peaks his head around the corner.

    “Everything okay, Zosh?”

    Zosia looks at him and smiles. “All good, Evan. Can you start prepping the garnishes and ice bins?”

    “You got it boss.” He disappears.

    “Your review wasn’t fair, Zosia,” Carol says after a moment, when Evan’s footsteps disappear. She has adjusted her tone now, only slightly, realizing that she is arguing with a brick wall. “You sampled a fraction of my menu.”

    Zosia stands up now and because she is already so close and so tall, Carol has no choice but to take a step back.

    “So what? You want me to change my review because…? You came here and you’re upset?” There is no malice in her voice, just questioning. “Is that what you want? Because if you want more stars then you need to earn them.” She steps closer again, like this is the beginning of a bad porno where Carol is begging her professor for a passing grade and the only way she’ll get it is with her ass bent over a barstool.

    Carol clenches her jaw so hard it could crack a tooth. Earn them. Like she is a fucking dog. “Tell me something, Wine Lady,” she says as she squares her shoulders. She feels ridiculous as she does it because Zosia is towering over her but it is too late so she has to double down. “What is it about my peculiar charm? Or is that just something you say to everyone to soften the blow?”

    “You know what La Ruche lacks, Carol?” Zosia says coolly, still very much unaffected by Carol’s comical display of anger. “A theme. Coherence. You have pretty dishes, soft lighting and a head chef with a nice face. That’s your brand but your food is trying to be everything and committing to nothing.”

    Zosia is flirting with her, she has to be, but Carol can’t prove it. She barrels her gaze into Zosia searching for something but she is not sure what.

    “You are so pretentious,” Carol says, pointing a finger at her and glancing at the stupid wolf on her t-shirt.

    “And you are talented. So if you want those extra three stars- ” Zosia reiterates, pressing one finger back into the space between Carol’s shoulder and chest, into the well worn leather, causing her to wobble slightly off balance and she is mildly offended by that, looks down at the finger and back up. “Impress me, then.” Another brief silence. “And Carol?”

    “What.”

    Zosia glances behind her. “You are getting a ticket.”

    -

    Carol pleads with the parking enforcement officer outside but to no avail. She is hyper aware that Zosia is watching from inside and she just about wants to bury her head into the ground. She can’t even bring herself to turn around but it doesn’t matter because she hears the door open and Zosia steps out onto the sidewalk with her helmet.

    “I think you’re going to need this.”

    Carol takes it, muttering a begrudging thanks. The officer leaves, handing her the ticket and she shoves it into her pocket like a used tissue before sliding onto her bike. Zosia follows, hovering next to her with her arms folded tightly against the cold.

    “I meant what I said,” Zosia says with a shiver as Carol slips her helmet on. “Those three stars could be yours if you want them badly enough.”

    Carol scoffs, but it is muted through the helmet. She is not a beggar and will never be a beggar and Zosia must be insane if she thinks Carol is going to grovel for a fucking Google review of all things.

    “Keep your stars,” Carol says, pulling her gloves on. She turns her key in the ignition and the bike rumbles loudly to life and Zosia takes a step back. “Later, Zosia,” she shouts as she flips her visor down and kicks up the kickstand.

    “Call me, Carol,” Zosia shouts back and Carol misses the way she smirks as she checks her blindspot.

    Carol simply shakes her head and pulls out into the street. She flashes Zosia a peace sign over her shoulder and in her side mirror, Zosia gives her that same stupid wave from last night. Why the fuck did she even come here.

    Maybe she will send Zosia the thank you card she so desires with the parking ticket inside and her email for an e-transfer.

    “Fucking Anthony Bourdain wannabe,” she grumbles to herself as she flies down King Street.

     

     

    Chapter 2: eat your words

    Chapter Summary

    There is nothing sexy about panko.

    Chapter Notes

    Zosia Nowak is mean.

    Or at least that is what some people have told her. Who are these people? Well, the people who have been on the receiving end of a poor review or article. The people who have worked for years only to allow her words, honest as they are, to dismantle said hard work. These are the people that simply could not hack it, who did not have their hearts in it.

    Because any self-respecting and passionate business owner or chef would have persevered and come out stronger. These people who call her mean and then fail? Well, she is just doing them a favour.

    Zosia Nowak is not mean though. 

    She is driven and her blinding confidence is off-putting to many people. Simply put, she knows what she wants and how to get it and that is exactly why The Wine Lady is the success that it is. That is why she is one of Toronto Life’s highest paid and most notable writers.

    She tells that to herself as she settles the till behind the bar.

    “You know she’s kind of a playboy, right?” The words are said a few minutes later.

    Zosia looks up from the wine glass she’s drying and then across the bar to Hannah, her bar manager and friend, who is wiping down a table. It’s just after 2:00am and The Wine Lady has been closed now for an hour, Evan and the rest of the staff already gone home. Her first unwarranted thought is the ungodly image of Hugh Hefner in a red robe and she makes a face.

    “Who is?” One eyebrow arches up and she sets the glass onto the counter with a soft clink, the ache in her feet and shoulders after a long day making itself known.

    “That blonde I saw you talking to earlier before we opened,” Hannah says, pushing in a chair. Her tone is matter-of-fact, as if Zosia should know who she is talking about. “Carol, I think her name is.”

    “Oh.” Zosia’s voice is sharp, surprised. She had not expected any of those words to come out of Hannah’s mouth because she hadn’t realized Hannah had even seen or heard any of that.

    Zosia tilts her head, picking up another glass to dry it. She mulls it over, the thought of Hannah and Carol knowing each other. They don’t exactly scream like they come from the same social circles, but then again, Zosia doesn’t actually know Carol. So. “Uh, yeah, that’s right. You know her?”

    Hannah shrugs, taking the seat across the bar in front of Zosia. “I know of her. She’s on every dating app possible. I recognized her dumb bike before I recognized her. She slept with two of my friends last summer and things got…messy.” A pause. “Separately,” she clarifies.

    “So that means you’re also on every dating app possible.”

    “Not the point.”

    Zosia hums, pondering it. She tosses her dish rag into a bin at the edge of the bar where it lands with a splat. It would be remiss of her to say she had not thought, several times, about the angry chef that had manifested in the middle of the bar earlier today. Then she tries to picture it. This pocket-sized, seething, gerbil-energied woman swiping her way through the city of Toronto- she cuts her thought short. There is no reason for Zosia to care about what a stranger does with their free time.

    “Wait,” Zosia says, tucking the last of the glasses away. “Why are you telling me this? What does her being a…playboy,” she cringes at the word, “have to do with anything?”

    “Oh, I just thought- it looked like- ” Hannah stops abruptly, realization settling on her face that she has probably misinterpreted what she had seen. She looks at Zosia like she has just been caught redhanded.

    Zosia simply folds her arms, looks back right at Hannah with half a smirk. “Go ahead, Han, finish that sentence.”

    After a second, Hannah gives in. “It looked like you were flirting.”

    Half a scoff leaves Zosia’s throat. “I was not flirting.” It comes out too quickly and defensively, but she hadn’t been. Really. At least not on purpose? Carol had appeared out of nowhere with zero warning and it had actually momentarily derailed Zosia. But Zosia also had not expected her to look the way she did. Which was, unfortunately, really fucking good. “I reviewed her restaurant. She came to…give me a piece of her mind.”

    Hannah barks out a laugh and slams both palms onto the bar. “A piece of her mind or a piece of her ass?”

    “Hannah.” Zosia already knows she has told her too much. Then, a little softer, even though Hannah never asked. “She called me pretentious.”

    “Oh, babe,” Hannah pouts, amused. “You are pretentious.”

    “Okay, rude? No I’m not.”

    “How much is your necklace worth, Zo?”

    Zosia places her hand over it, fingers brushing against the solid gold Byzantine chain, knowing that just one link of this necklace is worth more than one of Hannah’s paycheques. “That’s irrelevant and none of your business.”

    “Right, exactly.” Hannah is smug now but it is nothing Zosia is not used to. “So, let me guess. You gave her one star and some scathing comment.”

    “Two actually. And it wasn’t scathing. It was honest.”

    “I’ll be the judge of that.” Hannah reaches into her pocket for her phone. “What’s the name of the restaurant? It’s the one on The Esplanade right?”

    Zosia lets out a strangled breath, narrowing her eyes towards Hannah. How does she know so much about this woman? Before last night, Chef Carol Sturka had just been a name that had been recommended to her. Had Zosia Googled her? Of course, but that had been for purely professional reasons. She had not even known what this woman looked like.

    “La Ruche,” she mutters reluctantly as she turns around, busying herself with wiping down the back counter.

    Zosia can practically hear Hannah’s excitement as she pulls up the review and she already knows what the reaction will be because she has known Hannah since they were both 21, albeit on and off, and had been waiting tables together at Sneaky Dee’s. Since then, their relationship has consisted of working odd jobs together here and there. They had only crossed over into actual friendship last year when Hannah had reached out to Zosia and asked her for a job at The Wine Lady, which Zosia had gladly taken her up on because she really needed the help.

    “‘While they shot for the moon, they did not land among the stars,’” Hannah reads out loud with amusement. “Good lord, Zo. Are you trying to force her into early retirement?”

    “What?” Zosia spins around quickly, voice sharp. She has written hundreds of Google reviews. Countless articles. And many worse than this. She has sunk restaurants and bars. This is nothing. “That’s not that bad.”

    “If I were her, I’d start rethinking my entire career.”

    “Han, that’s really not- ”

    Hannah starts laughing and Zosia is just staring at her. “Her peculiar charm? Okay, so you were flirting.”

    “Oh my god,” Zosia groans, wants to reach out and smack the phone out of Hannah’s hands. “Are you fucking done? Can we leave now?”

    Hannah is still laughing and Zosia no longer has the energy. She rounds the bar, flicks off the lights, and leaves Hannah cackling in the dark.

    -

    Luna is already waiting by the door with her tail wagging when Zosia finally gets home.

    “Hi, baby!” She drops to her knees as Luna jumps up on her, licking her face. “Yes, yes, Oh, I missed you so much too! Did uncle Marek come feed you and take you for a walk?” She glances into the kitchen and there is kibble all over the floor and her leash is draped over the side of the couch instead of hanging in the closet where it usually is. So yes, her brother had been here. Her brother is usually here when Zosia knows she will be working late.

    The thing with Zosia is that she has been single longer than she might care to admit. It’s not that she can’t find anyone to date. She can, easily. The attention is there, always, she turns heads on the daily. It’s just that most people are not worth her time. Man or woman. The last man she had been with, that she thought might become her husband had only used her for her money. The last woman she had been with, well, had done the same but only in slightly less nefarious ways. And if they are not after her money or the privileges that come with being with a Nowak, they are only after one fun night.

    And Zosia will not give them that. Usually.

    So, last year when she had finally had enough, grown sick of the tired, repetitive dating scene and forcing fake connections, she had deleted all her dating apps and decided that a dog would be the answer. A dog, at least, would not try and screw her out of and take over her business and claim it as their own. A dog would not lie to her. And that is how she came upon Luna. The real love of her life.

    And while she may not have a partner to co-parent Luna with, she has her brother. Her best friend. Tragic in some ways, but Marek has also never tried to deplete half her savings account in the middle of the night because he had one drunken, half-formed idea that he could have a stake in the Toronto Blue Jays franchise.

    But that is neither here nor there.

    “Come on,” Zosia says, kissing Luna on the head and scratching her neck before urging her upstairs. “Let’s go to bed.”

    -

    Zosia is not sure why she does it. Maybe her conversation with Hannah last night is still fresh. Maybe the fact that a certain pair of blue eyes had lingered in the shadows of her thoughts all night. But the first thing she does when she wakes up, while she is still in bed with Luna curled at her side, is grab her phone, open Google, and type in ‘carol sturka.’

    And no, today is it not for professional reasons.

    The first link that pops up is La Ruche’s website. The link is already purple. Zosia scrolls past it.

    The next link that pops up is her LinkedIn. Also purple. Also scrolls.

    The third link is to Facebook. Still blue. She clicks it. It is the wrong Carol Sturka. This Carol Sturka looks to be well into her seventies and lives in Mobile, Alabama.

    The fourth link she finds is to Twitter. @i_am_chef_sturka. Surely that is too many underscores. Zosia clicks the link and confirms it’s her as per the profile picture. A semi professional headshot of her in a white chef’s coat. Plain background, tight smile, crease between her brows. Like she had been held at gunpoint to take the photo and still decided to use it anyways. Zosia snorts and rolls her eyes.

    The account is fucking private.

    Okay, so Carol Sturka does not have an online presence and something about that, despite Zosia knowing absolutely nothing about this woman other than the fact that she is apparently a ‘playboy’, just makes sense.

    Luna stretches long and lets out a big yawn. Zosia expects her to jump out of the bed and run downstairs, whining to be let out, but she doesn’t, just falls back asleep so Zosia affords herself a few extra minutes to stay in bed. It’s Sunday after all and she has very little to do today.

    Zosia closes Google and opens her Instagram. The first thing she does is check her personal account. Not much of note there. Marek had posted a photo of him and Luna on their walk last night. She likes the photo. Then she switches to @WineMe_DineMe_TO. As usual, her DMs are crowded but it’s all the usual fair. Just people responding and reacting to her posts and stories. After reading through some of them, she rewatches her own two stories from yesterday.

    The first one is a photo of a Nowak Estates vintage 2019 Chardonnay next to a small wooden platter of brie cheese and crackers sitting atop the bar at The Wine Lady with a caption that reads ‘the perfect pairing to get you in the mood for summer.’ When she swipes up to see who has viewed the story, one of the very first names that catches her eye after she passes through all the blue checkmarks is @sturka.carol. Oh. Zosia blinks once at it and can hear Carol’s voice calling her pretentious. Hot.

    She checks the next post. This one, a line-up of new craft beers from Bellwoods Brewery that they will be featuring in a partnership to help promote this summer’s Beer & Wine Fest that is slated to take place sometime in July. Carol is there too.

    Zosia shouldn’t be surprised. She had literally given Carol a business card with the Instagram account name on it, but she had demoralized Carol enough that Carol felt it warranted a surprise, indignant visit, so seeing her name there among the thousands who have also seen it, evokes some odd feeling inside her that she can’t quite place.

    So of course Zosia clicks on her name.

    Carol’s bio is just three emojis: a chef, a motorcycle, and most notably, the pride flag. Not that last night’s conversation had not already confirmed that. At least she is to the point.

    Her profile picture is the most amusing part. Just Carol, sitting on her motorcycle, sunglasses on, arms folded. It’s too small to see the details but Zosia finds herself snorting at it.

    And of course, the account is also private.

    For almost too long, Zosia stares at the blue ‘follow’ button knowing that she is not actually going to click it. She has no business trying to, what, make friends? with a woman who likely hates her. Who is probably making a list of all the different ways she might be able to burn down The Wine Lady, run her out of business, or poison her next meal if she ever found herself at La Ruche again.

    After a while, when she realizes how insane she is being, Zosia finally puts her phone away. A horrid smell just has left Luna’s body that tells her it’s time to take her out so in under four minutes, Zosia is dressed and downstairs with Luna in her harness and they are out the door.

    So for the rest of the week, this is just how it goes. Zosia posts a story on Instagram, more frequently than she normally would, and within the hour, Carol is there. And every time Zosia sees her name and tiny profile picture, she gets rewarded with the smallest rush of dopamine that has her feeling, at her big age of 37, like she is too fucking old for this shit.

    But she keeps doing it.

     

    *

     

    Eight days go by.

    In eight days, Carol does a normal amount things. She goes to work, goes to the gym, goes to St. Lawrence Market to stock her own kitchen, and when she has a moment of downtime, she dispiritedly swipes through her very unholy trinity of dating apps. Hinge, Bumble, and Tinder. She makes a handful of matches but nothing comes of them. Probably because she has been feeling off her game. Maybe leading a life of celibacy doesn’t sound so bad. Hah. Who is she kidding.

    In eight days, Carol has read and re-read The Wine Bitch’s two star review so many times she can recite it word for word with her eyes closed. Her favourite line is ‘each dish felt like it was having an identity crisis’ because of how loud and wrong it is. Carol is no stranger to what an identity crisis is. It’s what she had experienced in high school when she had started realizing what a raging dyke she was despite having a boyfriend.

    That is an identity crisis. Not miso buerre blanc.

    In those same eight days, Carol has spent every single one of them thinking about Zosia fucking Nowak.

    Her stupid wolf t-shirt. Her stupid gold chain. And her even stupider face.

    There are two reasons for this, other than this godforsaken review taking up every last remaining ounce of brain capacity she has.

    The first is that Carol rides past her bar twice a day, to and from work. Each time she does, she can’t help but glance inside. She had only seen her twice, but one of which was enough to make her almost rear-end the car in front of her.

    The second is because every day, like clockwork, Zosia posts some eye roll-inducing story on her Instagram. And every time, Carol, like a rat to a cheeto, is there. It’s always just food or drink, nothing that actually excites her.

    Except one day Zosia posts a photo of her and another man inside a brewhouse holding up unmarked cans of beer - the caption: limited time surprise batch coming your way! In that photo Zosia is in unbuttoned flannel and plain white cropped shirt that shows off way too much of her flat stomach and belly button. Her hair is up in a messy bun, and she is smiling wide. One thought flickers through Carol’s mind and it is that, with a heavy sigh, why does she have to be so goddamn fuckable? Carol zooms in for so long onto the picture that she nearly likes it.

    God. Can you imagine.

    In eight fucking long ass days, one thing that Carol does not do is change La Ruche’s menu. The menu is fine. It’s better than fine. It’s eclectic and diverse and up until now, nobody has ever complained. The theme is French fusion and will remain as such. She has discussed this entire ordeal with Koumba and Manny in aggravating detail and they are both in agreement that the menu does not need to change. 

    It’s not until Sunday night that something snaps in Carol and an idea, no doubt a terrible one, arrives to her.

    She’s on the couch watching an episode of Border Security and more than half a bottle of wine deep because the restaurant is closed tomorrow. Her feet are up on the coffee table and her hand is buried deep inside a box of Cinnamon Toast Crunch because god forbid sometimes even a classically trained, highly experienced chef wants to eat dry cereal straight out the box with their hands for dinner.

    The thing is, Carol has not been able to stop hearing all the different ways Zosia had called her mediocre. Every syllable, every written word lives in her frontal lobe on a never ending loop. In most cases she would have let this go by now, but this one she still cannot shake as much as she tries to. Maybe it’s because Zosia had looked so fucking bored the first time she said it.

    Carol will blame this on the alcohol later if she needs to but she leans forward, mutes the TV, and grabs the business card that’s been sitting on her coffee table all week and starts to type the number into her phone. She gets all the way this time, all ten digits.

    For a few seconds before she actually hits the call button, Carol can hear her own pulse. Then with a silent prayer to an empty room, she presses the green button.

    The phone rings for what feels like an eternity and Carol is halfway to hanging up when finally, someone answers.

    “Hello? Zosia Nowak speaking.”

    How formal. Carol hates it. She swallows and then,

    “What’s up, Wine Lady?”

    There’s a long pause. In the background, a dog barks. Luna, Carol assumes. Then there’s a quiet breath, almost a laugh, and Carol’s pulse has shifted into her throat.

    “Carol. Hi. To what do I owe this late night pleasure?”

    Carol looks at the time on the cable box under the TV. It’s not even that late. 9:15pm. God. Is she talking to a robot? At any consolation, if she is, she might the world’s hottest robot.

    “Just gonna cut to the chase here. What are you doing on Tuesday night?”

    “Are you asking me on a date?”

    So. Zosia has jokes. Okay.

    “Depends. Do you wanna go on one?”

    Another pause. Carol smirks and idly wonders what Zosia had been doing, why it took her three business days to answer the phone, what she’s wearing… There’s a sound of a proper swallow and a telltale soft clink of a wine glass being set onto granite. Aw, how cute, twins.

    Monday comes and goes in the blink of an eye.

    By midnight, they are literally covered in blood, sweat, and tears, and Carol is wondering why the fuck she thought this was a good idea.

    By 2:00am, Carol is finally in bed.

    She does not sleep.

    “On my bike.”

    —

    Thursday arrives in a blink.

    It is just after seven in the evening. Zosia is sitting in the wine cellar with Evan as they organize all the trivia questions into their categories on his laptop. He is going to host. Idly, Zosia wonders if Evan knows what happened down here, if Hannah had blabbed her big mouth to anyone else. If he does know, he has done a great job at not letting on because right now he is sitting exactly where- nevermind.

    Zosia looks at her phone then but it has been relatively quiet today. Not her usual notification after notification from Carol. She deflates when she sees there is still nothing from her. She opens their messages and last one is from nearly four hours ago, almost five now which had been a selfie of Zosia and Luna on the couch. Luna misses you is what the message says. There had been no reply, just a heart-eyes emoji on the photo.

    It’s fine, Zosia tells herself. Carol is busy at work and she knows that. It’s just that Carol has always found the time to text Zosia, even amidst kitchen fires and devastating spills. The silence is eating away at her.

    She types out a message. La Ruche must be busy today. She deletes it. Her phone goes screen down on the table with a sigh she does not mean to be audible.

    Evan glances at her. “You okay, Zosh?”

    Zosia just smiles at him. “I’m fine, Evan,” she says, giving him a squeeze on the shoulder.

    —

    By eight o’clock, the bar is already full with people who are here specifically for trivia, and others who have just wandered in unknowingly. Zosia, from behind the bar, scans her eyes around the room knowing that it’s too early for Carol to be here anyways.

    The phone goes back into her pocket and Zosia takes another round of orders.

    —

    It is 9:15pm when Evan starts tapping his finger on the mic.

    Zosia’s phone is still cold. She does not even care about the trivia anymore, she just wants to hear something, anything, from Carol.     Trivia night is in full swing.

    Still, Hannah, for her own good, does not say anything. She just places an arm around Zosia and gives her a tight squeeze and a very chaste kiss on the cheek.

    And Zosia, with a fake smile plastered on her face and barbed wire around her chest, has never felt more stupid.
